{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"7ce8881e-5fa8-4b31-a62a-9ff4d72be4e8","agentId":"a8f210f6f7381fe6e","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"Deep internet research (WebSearch + WebFetch extensively) on CRYPTOGRAPHIC PROVENANCE / TRANSPARENCY + SOVEREIGN / CONFIDENTIAL AI, 2023-2026. I'm designing a system whose findings + actions are written to a tamper-evident, Ed25519-signed, hash-chained log (already built in SIGIL, with a Merkle-prune head), and I want to push this to a world-class \"provable + sovereign\" trust story. I need the deepest available techniques to import.\n\nResearch and report on:\n1. **Transparency logs / verifiable append-only logs** — Certificate Transparency (RFC 6962, Merkle tree logs), **Sigstore / Rekor** (signed software transparency log), **Trillian** (Google's verifiable log), Go's **checkpoint / witness / gossip** model for split-view detection, **key transparency**. How they achieve tamper-evidence + auditability + split-view resistance. How our signed hash-chained spine could adopt these (witnessing, gossip, inclusion/consistency proofs) to become a publicly-verifiable pentest-evidence log.\n2. **Supply-chain provenance** — **in-toto**, **SLSA**, signed attestations, **C2PA / content provenance** (for AI outputs). How a \"proof-carrying finding\" could be a standard, verifiable attestation.\n3. **Confidential computing / TEEs** — running AI agents + secrets inside **TEEs** (Intel TDX, AMD SEV-SNP, AWS Nitro Enclaves, NVIDIA confidential GPUs), **remote attestation**, confidential inference. How a sovereign agent could prove it ran unmodified on trusted hardware.\n4. **Verifiable AI inference** — proving an LLM produced an output (zkML / proof-of-inference, or attested inference); is any of this practical yet? Signed model outputs.\n5. **Local-first / sovereign AI** — the local-first software movement, sovereign AI, self-hosted agent stacks, data sovereignty. What \"sovereign\" means technically and what the best local-first architectures do.\n6. **Threshold cryptography / multi-party control** — m-of-n signing (already in the codebase), threshold approval for high-risk actions, hardware co-signers (YubiKey/HSM), social recovery. For governing an autonomous offensive agent so no single point can authorize destruction.\n7. **Tamper-evident audit for autonomous agents** — any prior art on cryptographically-logged AI agent actions / accountable AI / auditable autonomy.\n\nFor each: technique, projects/standards/URLs, maturity, and how it plugs into our signed-spine + PCF-cert + WARDEN architecture.\n\nEND WITH: the 3-5 highest-leverage additions that would make our system's provenance + sovereignty story genuinely world-first (e.g., a witnessed transparency log of oracle-confirmed findings; TEE-attested sovereign agent; threshold-gated destructive actions). Cite URLs. Read this BEFORE running an engagement/scan, re-verifying a finding, inspecting the event spine, reasoning about the veracity/anti-hallucination layer, the learning/metacognition core, or the safety stack. Use it when a task names CRUCIBLE, OBSIDIAN, `python3 -m framework.v2 <subcommand>`, findings/oracles/evidence certificates, the blackboard event spine, critics, reflection, or the reward/calibration loop.\n- deep-research: Deep research harness — fan-out web searches, fetch sources, adversarially verify claims, synthesize a cited report. - When the user wants a deep, multi-source, fact-checked research report on any topic. BEFORE invoking, check if the question is specific enough to research directly — if underspecified (e.g., \"what car to buy\" without budget/use-case/region), ask 2-3 clarifying questions to narrow scope. Then pass the refined question as args, weaving the answers in.\n- adversarial-sweep: Independent multi-lens adversarial re-attack of a defensive-oracle domain — the FORGE stage-9 companion to RED-PEN. Attack from disjoint lenses, refute each objection with a real repro, report only what survives. - Before merging any AEGIS/FORGE domain stream, alongside (not instead of) the red-pen agent. Pass the domain spec via args. On the email-auth stream it caught 3 CRITICAL/LOW defects PAST a RED-PEN PASS — near-zero-FP cannot be self-certified by one reviewer.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- verify: Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.\n- code-review: Review the current diff for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud). Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. sigstore/rekor: Software Supply Chain Transparency Log · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/sigstore/rekor\"},{\"title\":\"An Introduction to Rekor — Chainguard Academy\",\"url\":\"https://edu.chainguard.dev/open-source/sigstore/rekor/an-introduction-to-rekor/\"},{\"title\":\"Rekor v2 GA - Cheaper to run, simpler to maintain - Sigstore Blog\",\"url\":\"https://blog.sigstore.dev/rekor-v2-ga/\"},{\"title\":\"Using rekor-monitor to Scan Your Transparency Logs - Sigstore Blog\",\"url\":\"https://blog.sigstore.dev/using-rekor-monitor/\"},{\"title\":\"Immutable Transparency Logs with Sigstore Rekor - alphasec\",\"url\":\"https://alphasec.io/immutable-transparency-logs-with-sigstore-rekor/\"},{\"title\":\"Announcing the Sigstore Transparency Log Research Dataset – Open Source Security Foundation\",\"url\":\"https://openssf.org/blog/2025/10/15/announcing-the-sigstore-transparency-log-research-dataset/\"},{\"title\":\"The AGNTCY Agent Directory Service: Architecture and Implementation\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.18787\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found regarding Sigstore Rekor's transparency log architecture, inclusion/consistency proofs, and Trillian in 2024:\n\n## Rekor v2 Architecture\n\nRekor v2 will be backed by a tile-based log and will use a modernized version of Trillian, Trillian-Tessera. Rekor v2 is a redesigned and modernized Rekor, Sigstore's signature transparency log, transitioning its backend to a tile-backed transparency log implementation to simplify maintenance and lower operational costs.\n\n## Inclusion and Consistency Proofs\n\nSigstore's transparency log is a SHA-256 Binary Merkle Tree with inclusion and consistency proofs supported. The log will return inclusion proofs on upload and these should be persisted alongside artifacts and their signatures.\n\nWitnessing provides independent verification that the log remains consistent (append-only). Witnessing can either be asynchronous, where a client requests witnesses verify consistency proofs, or synchronous, where the log requests witnesses verify proofs for every checkpoint issued.\n\n## 2024 Developments\n\nIn the initial launch of Rekor v2, checkpoints will not be witnessed, while we wait for the launch of a public witness network. Additionally, rekor-monitor can verify the consistency of the log and ensure that the transparency log has not been tampered with by computing a consistency proof using previous and current log checkpoints.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"38b9f53e-1837-4f2e-b588-084540c10e4a","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:36:23.500Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"fc205914-09b2-4875-9495-3da6025d5807","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

transparency-dev/witness: Libraries and binaries for running witnesses for verifiable logs · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/transparency-dev/witness\"},{\"title\":\"RFC 6962 - Certificate Transparency - Datatracker - IETF\",\"url\":\"https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc6962\"},{\"title\":\"RFC 6962: Certificate Transparency\",\"url\":\"https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6962.html\"},{\"title\":\"C2SP/tlog-witness.md at main · C2SP/C2SP\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/C2SP/C2SP/blob/main/tlog-witness.md\"},{\"title\":\"RFC 6962 - Certificate Transparency\",\"url\":\"https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/rfc6962/?include_text=1\"},{\"title\":\"Tamper-Evident Witness Logs: SOTA survey and gap analysis for RVM's witness chain (keyed-BLAKE3 + Merkle sealing) vs transparency logs, verifiable DBs, and secure-logging literature · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://gist.github.com/ruvnet/2737be4a2fe48dc2e4f31e9b00e988f0\"},{\"title\":\"RFC 6962\",\"url\":\"https://www.ietf.org/rfc/rfc6962.txt\"}]\n\nHere are the search results for your query about Certificate Transparency RFC 6962 with checkpoint witness gossip split-view detection and tlog-witness:\n\n## Key Findings:\n\n**RFC 6962 Foundation:**\nViolation of the append-only property is detected by global gossiping, i.e., everyone auditing logs comparing their versions of the latest Signed Tree Heads. All clients should gossip with each other, exchanging STHs at least; this is all that is required to ensure that they all have a consistent view.\n\n**Witnesses and Split-View Detection:**\nA witness is an entity which helps to prevent split-view attacks by transparency logs (tlogs). It does this by verifying that tlogs are evolving in an append-only manner, and counter-signs checkpoints that represent an append-only evolution from any previously witnessed checkpoints. The witness is an HTTP service that stores checkpoints it has seen from different verifiable logs in a sqlite database. This is a very lightweight way to help detect or even prevent split-view attacks.\n\n**tlog-witness Specification:**\nThis repository contains libraries and binaries for running witnesses which comply with the tlog-witness spec and can integrate with the public witness network. The consistency proof lines MUST encode a Merkle Consistency Proof from the old size to the checkpoint size according to RFC 6962, Section 2.1.2.\n\n**Modern Implementation:**\nA quorum of witnesses defends against split view attacks. Can reuse improved current generation of logs (Tiled Transparency Logs) with a collection of immutable, cacheable resources.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"ba9e0c87-4edb-459f-84fe-0662ec04eafe","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:36:28.543Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0f092f08-3638-4c69-b9dd-4f6c89734d3e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Apple Security Research\",\"url\":\"https://security.apple.com/blog/imessage-contact-key-verification/\"},{\"title\":\"Bringing more transparency to post-quantum usage, encrypted messaging, and routing security\",\"url\":\"https://blog.cloudflare.com/radar-origin-pq-key-transparency-aspa/\"},{\"title\":\"WhatsApp and Cloudflare enhance security by auditing key transparency for end-to-end encrypted messages | WABetaInfo\",\"url\":\"https://wabetainfo.com/whatsapp-and-cloudflare-enhance-security-by-auditing-key-transparency-for-end-to-end-encrypted-messages/\"},{\"title\":\"Overview · Cloudflare Key Transparency Auditor docs\",\"url\":\"https://developers.cloudflare.com/key-transparency/\"},{\"title\":\"User Perceptions of Key Transparency in WhatsApp\",\"url\":\"https://petsymposium.org/popets/2025/popets-2025-0170.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"Techniques for peer-to-peer key verification\",\"url\":\"https://image-ppubs.uspto.gov/dirsearch-public/print/downloadPdf/12368576\"},{\"title\":\"VCT: A Verifiable Transcript System for LLM Conversations\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.23003\"},{\"title\":\"TAP: Transparent and Privacy-Preserving Data Services\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2210.11702\"},{\"title\":\"RWC 2024 - WhatsApp Key Transparency\",\"url\":\"https://iacr.org/submit/files/slides/2024/rwc/rwc2024/18/slides.pdf\"}]\n\nHere are the search results for your query about key transparency protocol, WhatsApp, and Apple in 2024:\n\n## Key Findings\n\n**Key Transparency Protocol Overview:**\nKey Transparency (KT) builds on the ideas of Certificate Transparency but uses a verifiable log-backed map data structure, which can provide cryptographic proofs of inclusion and be audited for consistency over time.\n\n**WhatsApp's Implementation:**\nWhatsApp was the first to deploy a KT system for messaging called Auditable Key Directory (AKD). This system significantly improves scalability by batching map revisions as the key directory service inserts identifiers and keys, and employs Verifiable Random Functions to prevent auditors from recognizing user identifiers.\n\n**Apple's Approach:**\nApple is launching Key Transparency with an internal auditing service across its production KT trees, and plans to share more details about its public auditing strategy in 2024.\n\n**Third-Party Auditing:**\nIn September 2024, Cloudflare announced a Key Transparency auditor for WhatsApp, providing an independent verification layer that helps ensure the integrity of public key distribution for the messaging app's billions of users.\n\n**Verifiable Append-Only Log:**\nCloudflare's Key Transparency Auditor achieves security by building a verifiable append-only data structure called a Log, similar to Certificate Transparency.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"e703ff85-d1f9-41a2-a72e-6d4d3a272624","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:36:29.255Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7dd944ec-328a-46f1-a873-1c8396a2c341","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"0c2f85a2-330c-469c-8f7e-0e84d49225db","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"7ce8881e-5fa8-4b31-a62a-9ff4d72be4e8","agentId":"a8f210f6f7381fe6e","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014EWoLab7eX6ZsF4QfaNWVE","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"in-toto SLSA attestation framework provenance software supply chain 2024 2025\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"How we use software provenance at Red Hat | Red Hat Developer\",\"url\":\"https://developers.redhat.com/articles/2025/05/15/how-we-use-software-provenance-red-hat\"},{\"title\":\"Understanding Software Provenance Attestation: The Roles of SLSA and in-toto - Opera Omnia\",\"url\":\"https://mikael.barbero.tech/blog/post/2023-12-28-slsa-and-in-toto/\"},{\"title\":\"Human-Certified Module Repositories for the AI Age\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.02512\"},{\"title\":\"SLSA • Frequently asked questions\",\"url\":\"https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.1/faq\"},{\"title\":\"Deep Dive Into SLSA Provenance and Software Attestation\",\"url\":\"https://www.legitsecurity.com/blog/slsa-provenance-blog-series-part-2-deeper-dive-into-slsa-provenance\"},{\"title\":\"Artifact Provenance and Attestations: From SLSA to in-toto | Secure Pipelines\",\"url\":\"https://secure-pipelines.com/ci-cd-security/artifact-provenance-attestations-slsa-in-toto/\"},{\"title\":\"Analyzing Challenges in Deployment of the SLSA Framework for Software   Supply Chain Security\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.05014\"},{\"title\":\"SLSA • in-toto and SLSA\",\"url\":\"https://slsa.dev/blog/2023/05/in-toto-and-slsa\"},{\"title\":\"Overview | Harness Developer Hub\",\"url\":\"https://developer.harness.io/docs/software-supply-chain-assurance/artifact-security/slsa/overview/\"}]\n\n## Search Results Summary\n\nI found comprehensive information about in-toto, SLSA, and their roles in software supply chain security. Here are the key findings:\n\n### Framework Relationship\n\nThe SLSA specification recommends in-toto attestations as the vehicle to express Provenance and other attributes of software supply chains, with in-toto serving as the unopinionated layer to express information pertaining to a software supply chain, and SLSA as the opinionated layer specifying exactly what information must be captured to achieve guarantees of a particular level.\n\n### in-toto Framework\n\nin-toto is a framework to secure software supply chains hosted at the Cloud Native Computing Foundation that provides a generalized workflow to secure different steps in a software supply chain. in-toto is centered around software attestations, focusing on the generation and verification of metadata of the software supply chain, where developers and stakeholders generate metadata reflecting actions like coding and testing, and the project owner creates a layout that defines the supply chain's expected steps and corresponding metadata.\n\n### SLSA Framework\n\nSupply Chain Levels for Software Artifacts (SLSA) is an end-to-end security framework designed to enhance the integrity and security of software artifacts throughout the software supply chain, offering a checklist of standards and controls for preventing tampering, securing packages, and safeguarding infrastructure involved in software development and distribution.\n\n### Recent Developments (2024-2025)\n\nRed Hat produces in-toto attestations from the control plane and uses those to gate artifacts with machine-readable policies. Additionally, SLSA Provenance, the wider in-toto Attestation Framework, and various SBOM standards are all rapidly evolving spaces with ongoing investigation into linking between different formats and exploration of alignment on common models.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"2a30e54b-9d97-43ee-add7-bf93a6159be6","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:36:29.861Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0c2f85a2-330c-469c-8f7e-0e84d49225db","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Content Provenance Explained (2026)\",\"url\":\"https://c2paviewer.com/articles/what-is-c2pa\"},{\"title\":\"Industrialized Deception: The Collateral Effects of LLM-Generated Misinformation on Digital Ecosystems\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.21963\"},{\"title\":\"A Faceted Proposal for Transparent Attribution of AI-Assisted Text Production\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.25346\"},{\"title\":\"Strengthening Multimedia Integrity in the Generative AI Era\",\"url\":\"https://media.defense.gov/2025/Jan/29/2003634788/-1/-1/0/CSI-CONTENT-CREDENTIALS.PDF\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about C2PA content provenance and AI-generated content credentials:\n\n## C2PA Overview\n\nThe C2PA specification for Content Credentials provides a standardized method for attaching Content Credentials, which can be validated, to digital assets. The Coalition for Content Provenance and Authenticity (C2PA) was formed to address challenges by developing an open standard that enables creators, publishers, and consumers to verify the origins and history of digital content.\n\n## AI-Generated Content Identification\n\nEach action performed on an asset can be recorded in the asset's Content Credential, performed by a human, organization, or AI/ML system, and when performed by an AI/ML system, it is clearly identified as such through its digitalSourceType field.\n\n## Current Specification Versions\n\nThe search results indicate that there are multiple recent versions:\n- The most recent version as of early 2024 was 2.0, released in January 2024\n- Version 2.2 was released in May 2025 with added video streaming support, extended file format coverage, and updated Trust List infrastructure\n- C2PA Specification v2.3 (December 2025) introduced support for live video streaming and manifests for unstructured text\n\nNote: The search results show versions 2.2 and 2.3 as the most current, rather than 2.1. The 2.2 specification appears to be the current stable release as of May 2025.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"345a0dfa-b005-4b91-8c77-cc31322b7855","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:36:30.793Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0f657414-18db-4a3f-bed8-c5c8e7a9ae9c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Here are the key findings about confidential computing technologies and remote attestation:\n\n## Overview of Technologies\n\nAMD SEV-SNP, Intel TDX, AWS Nitro Enclaves, and NVIDIA GPU CC have different architectures, different shared resource boundaries, and different attestation mechanisms.\n\n## Remote Attestation\n\nAttestation is how a TEE proves to a remote party that it is running expected code on genuine hardware. Users may further assert the TEE hardware and enclosed-application authenticity and establish a secure communication channel thereto via remote attestation.\n\n## Recent Security Findings (2025)\n\nA significant security development emerged in 2025: The TEE.Fail attack (ACM CCS 2025) used a $1,000 device soldered to the DDR5 memory bus to extract attestation keys from Intel TDX and AMD SEV-SNP. This highlights concerns about physical access and hardware root-of-trust vulnerabilities.\n\n## Practical Use Cases (2025)\n\nOpen Enclave SDK, Gramine, Occlum, SCONE, Enarx, Fortanix EDP, and AWS Nitro SDKs have sufficiently robust runtimes, with containerized workflows. Enclaves are no longer exotic. They're appropriate for services that must handle plaintext secrets while operating in adversarial infrastructure.\n\n## Technology Comparison\n\nAMD SEV-SNP excels in scenarios requiring lower performance overhead and simpler implementation, making it ideal for financial services and healthcare applications. Intel TDX provides superior security assurance and comprehensive attestation capabilities, making it the preferred choice for government, defense, and high-security AI workloads.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"ba3c7728-0b78-46d4-af2c-4a0fbfbd2453","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:36:30.863Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a695d9ca-2c63-4613-bbea-f365bb95976c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

TEEs\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.31408\"}]\n\nI've successfully performed a web search for your query. Here are the key findings about NVIDIA H100 confidential computing, GPU attestation, and confidential inference:\n\n## H100 Confidential Computing Overview\n\nThe NVIDIA H100 Tensor Core GPU is the first GPU to support confidential computing, using a hardware-based trusted execution environment (TEE) anchored in an on-die hardware root of trust to protect code and data. NVIDIA introduced the first commercial GPU-CC solution as part of its Hopper architecture.\n\n## Attestation and GPU Security\n\nThe GPU generates an attestation report, a cryptographically signed document providing evidence of the GPU's state and the integrity of its operational environment. When it boots in CC-On mode, the GPU enables hardware protections for code and data.\n\n## Data Protection in Inference\n\nNVIDIA H100 GPUs operating in confidential computing mode work with CPUs that support confidential VMs, using an encrypted bounce buffer to move data between the CPU and GPU, ensuring secure data transfers and isolation against various threat vectors.\n\n## Performance and 2024-2025 Development\n\nConfidential computing architecture for AI inference in 2026 includes composite attestation across CPU TEE and NVIDIA H100/H200 GPU Confidential Computing, with 1-7 percent inference throughput overhead. Modern TEEs extend beyond CPUs to GPUs (NVIDIA H100/H200 Confidential Computing), allowing organizations to train and deploy AI models on sensitive data without exposing it to cloud providers, administrators, or attackers.\n\nThe research results also include recent academic work from 2024-2026 examining the security architecture of NVIDIA GPU confidential computing in detail.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"ce783108-f94a-4022-a5b2-c830ff153457","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:36:57.406Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b5a7ac45-1425-46d8-b314-682093b25866","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

ICME Labs Blog\",\"url\":\"https://blog.icme.io/the-definitive-guide-to-zkml-2025/\"},{\"title\":\"ZKML: Verifiable Machine Learning using Zero-Knowledge Proof - Joo Yeon Cho - Kudelski Security\",\"url\":\"https://kudelskisecurity.com/modern-ciso-blog/zkml-verifiable-machine-learning-using-zero-knowledge-proof\"},{\"title\":\"Zero-Knowledge Proofs for ML Inference\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/zero-knowledge-proofs-of-ml-model-inference\"},{\"title\":\"ZKTorch: Compiling ML Inference to Zero-Knowledge Proofs via Parallel Proof Accumulation\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.07031\"}]\n\nI've found comprehensive search results on Zero-Knowledge Machine Learning (zkML) with information about practical implementations in 2025 and the specific systems you mentioned.\n\n## Key Findings:\n\n**Survey and Scope:**\nZKML research published from June 2017 to August 2025 organizes existing studies into three core tasks: verifiable training, verifiable testing, and verifiable inference.\n\n**zkLLM System:**\nzkLLM is a zero-knowledge proof for language models that guarantees a language model produced an output without sharing any model details, making it self-contained and hard to forge with strong guarantees. For Transformer Neural Networks like GPT-2, proof generation time was 287.1 seconds, a 35.7× improvement over ZKML.\n\n**EZKL System:**\nEZKL is a library and command-line tool for doing inference for deep learning models and computational graphs in a zk-snark, allowing users to create ZK proofs of ML models exported using ONNX. EZKL uses Halo2 as its underlying proof system.\n\n**Practical Applications (2025):**\nONNX models can be converted into ZK circuits using EZKL or DeepProve. Recent systems claim to be 54-158x faster than EZKL and were first to prove a complete GPT-2 inference with verification times of 671x faster for MLPs and 521x faster for CNNs.\n\n**Ongoing Challenges:**\nDespite significant progress, proving times for the largest transformer/LLMs remain in the minutes to hours per inference range.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"471bb030-45a2-48b8-9a70-69571bd2549e","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:36:59.281Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b47f7ba8-930c-40cf-8d79-bb61736074e0","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

An Architecture for Trustworthy and Transparent Digital Supply Chains\",\"url\":\"https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-scitt-architecture/\"},{\"title\":\"draft-ietf-scitt-architecture-22\",\"url\":\"https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-scitt-architecture-22\"},{\"title\":\"draft-ietf-scitt-architecture-16 - An Architecture for Trustworthy and Transparent Digital Supply Chains\",\"url\":\"https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-scitt-architecture/16/\"},{\"title\":\"An Architecture for Trustworthy and Transparent Digital Supply Chains\",\"url\":\"https://ietf-wg-scitt.github.io/draft-ietf-scitt-architecture/draft-ietf-scitt-architecture.html\"},{\"title\":\"Supply Chain Integrity, Transparency, and Trust (SCITT)\",\"url\":\"https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/bofreq-birkholz-supply-chain-integrity-transparency-and-trust-scitt/\"},{\"title\":\"SCITT: Supply Chain Integrity, Transparency and Trust | Conserver.io\",\"url\":\"https://www.conserver.io/deep-dives/scitt-supply-chain-integrity-transparency-and-trust\"},{\"title\":\"What Is SCITT - SCITT - Supply Chain Integrity and Trust\",\"url\":\"https://scitt.io/\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, here's what I found about SCITT:\n\n## Overview\n\nSCITT is an architecture that defines a base set of extensible message structures and flows to make signed content transparent via verifiable data structures maintained by transparency services, with the goal of enhancing auditability and accountability for single-issuer signed content about supply chain commodities.\n\n## Key Components\n\n**Signed Statements and Receipts:**\nOnce a signed statement is registered, the transparency service issues a receipt, just as a notary stamps a document being notarized. The SCITT ledger represents a linear and irrevocable history of statements made.\n\n**Transparency Services:**\nTransparency services confirm a policy is met before recording the statement on the ledger.\n\n## Technical Implementation\n\nSCITT builds upon CBOR Object Signing and Encryption (COSE) for signature formats, ensuring compatibility with existing public key infrastructure while providing flexibility for future cryptographic algorithms. The framework uses hash chains similar to certificate transparency logs, creating an immutable record that makes tampering immediately detectable.\n\n## Purpose\n\nSCITT is a set of proposed IETF internet standards for managing the compliance of goods and services across end-to-end supply chains, supporting the ongoing verification where the authenticity of entities, evidence, policy, and artifacts can be assured and actions can be guaranteed to be authorized, non-repudiable, immutable, and auditable.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"84b5e761-0425-4687-af6c-d1d5e93b6f7b","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:36:59.937Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"267d491d-b402-48e8-b5a8-cc5057ecd849","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

FinanceFeeds\",\"url\":\"https://financefeeds.com/multi-party-computation-mpc-threshold-signatures/\"},{\"title\":\"Threshold Signatures and Multi-Party Computation (MPC) - Token Tool Hub – AI-Powered Crypto Tools & DeFi Insights\",\"url\":\"https://tokentoolhub.com/threshold-signatures-multi-party-computation-mpc/\"},{\"title\":\"mpc multi party computation | Cube Exchange\",\"url\":\"https://www.cube.exchange/what-is/mpc-multi-party-computation\"},{\"title\":\"TALUS: Threshold ML-DSA with One-Round Online Signing via Boundary Clearance and Carry Elimination\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.22109\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - near/threshold-signatures: Threshold signature schemes for the NEAR MPC system. · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/near/threshold-signatures\"},{\"title\":\"Secure Multiparty Computation of Threshold Signatures Made More Efficient\",\"url\":\"https://www.ndss-symposium.org/wp-content/uploads/2024-601-paper.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"Multi-Party Threshold Cryptography | CSRC | CSRC\",\"url\":\"https://csrc.nist.gov/projects/threshold-cryptography\"}]\n\nI found comprehensive information about threshold signatures, FROST, and MPC signing. Here are the key findings from the search:\n\n## Threshold Signatures and FROST\n\nFROST (Flexible Round-Optimized Schnorr Threshold signatures) is a cutting-edge threshold signature scheme designed for efficiency and security. FROST minimizes network overhead by reducing the number of communication rounds required for signing, making it suitable for distributed systems with unreliable networks.\n\n## Multi-Party Computation and M-of-N Signing\n\nA threshold signature scheme is a special case of a multiparty computation (MPC) functionality. MPC is a generic mechanism to securely partition a general functionality among a set of players so that all players must participate to perform the operation, and up to some subset of players is assumed to be corrupted. An MPC operation can be performed without parties revealing their inputs to each other while still issuing a valid output created from each of their respective (private) inputs.\n\n## 2024 Standards and Updates\n\nFROST was published as RFC 9591 in June 2024, titled \"Two-round threshold Schnorr signatures with FROST.\"\n\n## HSM Integration\n\nCombining secure enclaves, HSMs, or TEEs with MPC can reduce latency and improve tamper resistance.\n\n## Practical Applications\n\nIn cryptocurrency custody, companies like BitGo and Fireblocks use threshold signatures to protect assets by distributing signing authority among multiple parties, reducing the risk of single-point key theft.\n\nThe search results did not contain specific information about YubiKey integration with these technologies in 2024.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"0411036c-54d5-4cbc-b261-ed59d8ca9dd1","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:37:00.651Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a46c7a51-5de2-4746-9d25-4c8f32e4a4a4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

| Cloudera\",\"url\":\"https://www.cloudera.com/resources/faqs/sovereign-ai.html\"}]\n\nI've successfully performed a web search on \"local-first software sovereign AI self-hosted agent data sovereignty 2025\". Here are the key findings:\n\n## Core Concepts\n\nSovereign AI is an implementation of digital sovereignty that aims to decentralize AI capabilities by removing reliance on external gatekeepers, using open source models and local infrastructure as a framework that imagines AI as a locally owned and operated utility.\n\n## Local-First AI Agents\n\nLocal-first AI agents offer a path to full automation with full sovereignty. What is new is the combination of persistent autonomy and local execution, with these agents running scheduled jobs, reacting to events, maintaining long-term memory, and taking real actions across platforms like Slack, GitHub, and Telegram.\n\n## Data Sovereignty Imperatives\n\nAccording to a 2025 Cisco report, 92% of organizations cite data privacy as a top concern when adopting AI tools, making this local-first model increasingly relevant. According to the Deloitte 2025 AI Report, 68% of European financial institutions identify privacy as the main barrier to AI adoption.\n\n## Four Pillars of Sovereign AI\n\nTo truly achieve sovereignty, an entity must control four distinct pillars: data sovereignty (ensuring data is collected, stored, and processed according to local laws without unauthorized access by foreign entities), infrastructure sovereignty (running workloads on local hardware), model sovereignty (developing models trained on local languages and proprietary datasets), and software sovereignty (utilizing open-source or locally developed tools to prevent vendor lock-in).\n\n## 2025 Regulatory Developments\n\nThe UK established a Sovereign AI Unit in July 2025 with up to £500 million in funding to build national AI capability and security. Canada has launched a Canadian Sovereign AI Compute Strategy with $2 billion over five years to boost domestic AI compute capacity, with three components: mobilising private investment (up to $700M), building public supercomputing infrastructure, and an AI Compute Access Fund for researchers and companies.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"6e9ab13f-cebe-46cb-9f7b-e4c4608bf364","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:37:00.935Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b5432688-413b-4d3d-82ec-2f1aa9e634b2","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"8e13ee70-9eb0-46d4-9727-8e5316779207","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"7ce8881e-5fa8-4b31-a62a-9ff4d72be4e8","agentId":"a8f210f6f7381fe6e","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VbgXpSN15hQtff7A6QJ2rk","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"tamper-evident audit log autonomous AI agent accountable auditable agent actions cryptographic 2025\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Agent Action Audit Trail — Agentic AI AI Governance Control | AI Governance Institute\",\"url\":\"https://aigovernance.com/controls/agent-action-audit-trail\"},{\"title\":\"Audit Trails for AI Agents: Complete, Tamper-Proof Logging | Blck Alpaca\",\"url\":\"https://blckalpaca.at/en/knowledge-base/ai-agents/ai-agent-security-owasp/audit-trails-fuer-agents\"},{\"title\":\"Building a Secure AI Agent Audit Trail with Identiclaw\",\"url\":\"https://www.vouched.id/learn/blog/building-secure-ai-agent-audit-trail-identiclaw\"},{\"title\":\"Distributional AGI Safety\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.16856\"},{\"title\":\"Why Agentic AI Requires an Independent Audit Trail for Governance v.2 | by Valdez Ladd | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/@oracle_43885/architecting-trust-by-implementing-audit-parallels-in-agentic-ai-d82fb6f1030a\"},{\"title\":\"Trustworthy AI Agents: Verifiable Audit Logs | Sakura Sky: Cloud, Data, Security\",\"url\":\"https://www.sakurasky.com/blog/missing-primitives-for-trustworthy-ai-part-5/\"},{\"title\":\"Tamper-Evident Audit Logs for AI: What Cryptographic Integrity Brings to Compliance Records | DeepInspect\",\"url\":\"https://www.deepinspect.ai/blog/tamper-evident-audit-logs-for-ai\"},{\"title\":\"What Really Happened In There? A Tamper-Evident Audit Trail for AI Agents | nono\",\"url\":\"https://nono.sh/blog/secure-agent-audit\"},{\"title\":\"AI agents: building trust via cryptographic proof\",\"url\":\"https://www.aiacceleratorinstitute.com/verifiable-execution-for-ai-agents/\"},{\"title\":\"Tamper-Evident Audit Trails for AI Agents: What SIEM Integration Actually Requires\",\"url\":\"https://www.kiteworks.com/regulatory-compliance/ai-agent-audit-trail-siem-integration/\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found on tamper-evident audit logs for autonomous AI agents in 2025-2026:\n\n## Overview\n\nAn AI agent audit trail is a chronological, tamper-evident record of every agent input, reasoning step, tool call, and output. Tamper-evident action logs enable post-hoc accountability and incident investigation for agent-driven workflows.\n\n## Key Requirements\n\nAn audit trail must capture at least eight data points per agent action: the full prompt, the model version and configuration hash, the tool-call sequence with arguments, the retrieval queries and document IDs, the output together with its decision rationale, human-override events, memory read/write operations, as well as cost and latency.\n\nAgent logs should be treated with the same integrity requirements as financial audit logs: write-once, tamper-evident storage, access-controlled.\n\n## Cryptographic Implementation\n\nA genuine AI agent audit trail is a cryptographically sealed record of an agent's inputs, reasoning, tool use, and outputs. This could be achieved by recording transaction and decision logs in a cryptographically secured, append-only ledger, where entries are hashed and chained to ensure immutability.\n\nThe technique combines per-record signing, hash chaining, and external anchoring.\n\n## Regulatory Context\n\nRegulatory pressure from the EU AI Act is pushing organizations to demonstrate tamper-resistant logs and reproducibility for high-risk AI uses. EU AI Act Article 12, Fannie Mae LL-2026-04, HIPAA, DORA, and NIST AI RMF all expect records that an auditor can rely on as evidence.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"fa1982f7-816b-4a6e-bb43-dc24e1e0bff1","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:37:01.277Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8e13ee70-9eb0-46d4-9727-8e5316779207","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Chutes\",\"url\":\"https://chutes.ai/news/private-ai-inference-verifiable-confidential-llm-serving\"},{\"title\":\"Private AI Inference — Confidential LLM Serving | Phala\",\"url\":\"https://phala.com/solutions/private-ai-inference\"},{\"title\":\"Bit-Exact AI Inference Verification Without Performance Tradeoffs\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.00279\"},{\"title\":\"The Proxy Knows Too Much: Sealing LLM API Routers with Attested TEEs\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16358\"},{\"title\":\"Making AI-Assisted Grant Evaluation Auditable without Exposing the Model\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.25200\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, I found several relevant developments in attested inference and verifiable AI from 2025-2026:\n\n## Key Findings\n\n**Anthropic's Confidential Inference Work:**\nAnthropic published work on \"Confidential inference via trusted virtual machines\" in June 2025. This was a joint publication with Irregular (formerly Pattern Labs), and represents one of the cleaner public treatments of making AI trust verifiable rather than asserted.\n\n**Verifiable LLM Outputs:**\nMicrosoft Azure's Confidential AI Inferencing service runs an LLM endpoint inside a TDX virtual machine and provides publicly verifiable attestation policy that specifies which model, runtime, and system prompt versions are approved. Anthropic has published a technical approach to confidential inference that extends this model to include prompt privacy and output non-disclosure guarantees.\n\n**Attested Inference Infrastructure:**\nTEE attestations and cryptographic signing ensure that inference results are authentic and untampered, with every inference cryptographically signed and traceable on-chain. Routing requests to OpenAI, Anthropic, and other LLM providers is available through secure, attested connections.\n\n**Agent Verification:**\nThe conversation extends from confidential inference to attested agent identity.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"a599ecac-82ec-4af0-956a-0cdc0049b8cc","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:37:43.173Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"56170ba1-bc3c-4faf-a371-1864fcc8d25e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

The Go Programming Language\",\"url\":\"https://go.dev/blog/module-mirror-launch\"},{\"title\":\"I Built a New Certificate Transparency Log in 2024 - Here’s What I Learned\",\"url\":\"https://blog.transparency.dev/i-built-a-new-certificate-transparency-log-in-2024-heres-what-i-learned\"},{\"title\":\"transparency-dev · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/transparency-dev\"},{\"title\":\"Introducing Trillian Tessera: Tile-based Transparency Logs\",\"url\":\"https://blog.transparency.dev/introducing-trillian-tessera\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - transparency-dev/tessera: Go library for building tile-based transparency logs (tlogs) · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/transparency-dev/tessera\"},{\"title\":\"Tile-Based Transparency Logs | Trillian\",\"url\":\"https://transparency.dev/articles/tile-based-logs/\"},{\"title\":\"tlog package - golang.org/x/mod/sumdb/tlog - Go Packages\",\"url\":\"https://pkg.go.dev/golang.org/x/mod/sumdb/tlog\"},{\"title\":\"tessera package - github.com/transparency-dev/trillian-tessera - Go Packages\",\"url\":\"https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/transparency-dev/trillian-tessera\"},{\"title\":\"Building Efficient Verifiable Logs: Introducing Trillian Tessera and TesseraCT :: PTS2025 :: pretalx\",\"url\":\"https://cfp.pass-the-salt.org/pts2025/talk/WLKAH9/\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found:\n\n## Key Findings\n\n**Tessera Overview:**\nTessera is a tile-based transparency log library designed to improve the experience of log-operators running logs in any transparency space. As the next evolution of Trillian, Trillian Tessera implements tile-based logs to simplify and reduce the cost associated with building and operating transparency logs.\n\n**Connection to Go Checksum Database:**\nThe checksum database is served by sum.golang.org, and is built on a Transparent Log (or \"Merkle tree\") of hashes backed by Trillian. The tiles approach has been tested and proven in ecosystems such as the Go module checksum database and Pixel binary transparency.\n\n**Verifiable Log Technology:**\nThe same transparency log technology as the Go Checksum Database keeps operators honest and unable to misbehave. Tessera makes the log readable via the tlog-tiles API.\n\n**Recent Developments:**\nThe TrustFabric team at Google announced that Tessera, the C2SP tlog-tiles compliant transparency log library, is now generally available. Tessera was introduced at the Transparency.Dev summit in October 2024.\n\nTrillian Tessera is an open-source Go library for building tile-based transparency logs using these standard formats on both major cloud and on-premises infrastructure.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"3d9cd9d1-7a44-45e6-8fb6-0df7b07ebfb6","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:37:46.076Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"60f91a1e-f9fd-4f78-ac73-54f4a55962f8","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Structure, Assertions, and Verification\",\"url\":\"https://c2paviewer.com/articles/what-is-c2pa-manifest\"},{\"title\":\"How C2PA Content Credentials Work and What Their Limits Are - SoftwareSeni\",\"url\":\"https://www.softwareseni.com/how-c2pa-content-credentials-work-and-what-their-limits-are/\"},{\"title\":\"A Faceted Proposal for Transparent Attribution of AI-Assisted Text Production\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.25346\"},{\"title\":\"C2PA Standard: History, Promises and Structural Limitations\",\"url\":\"https://truescreen.io/articles/c2pa-standard-history-limitations/\"},{\"title\":\"International AI Safety Report 2025: Second Key Update: Technical Safeguards and Risk Management\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.19863\"},{\"title\":\"More Human or More AI? Visualizing Human-AI Collaboration Disclosures in Journalistic News Production\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.11072\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found regarding C2PA, text manifest support, and AI output attribution:\n\n## Key Findings\n\n**C2PA Support for Unstructured Text:**\n\nC2PA Specification v2.3 (December 2025) introduced support for live video streaming and manifests for unstructured text, extending provenance beyond media files to LLM outputs. Additionally, comprehensive support for embedding C2PA manifests in unstructured text files has been added, with unstructured text assets using Unicode Variation Selectors to embed C2PA Manifests as a sequence of non-rendering Unicode characters.\n\n**C2PA Manifest Structure:**\n\nC2PA Manifests contain one or more assertions, which are statements about the asset, such as its origin (i.e., when and where it was created), modifications (i.e., what happened using what tools) and use of AI (i.e., how it was authored).\n\n**AI Output Attribution:**\n\nAdobe Firefly, Stability AI, and OpenAI DALL-E all embed C2PA manifests with AI generation assertions on supported outputs, as of 2025.\n\n**C2PA's Role:**\n\nIt's important to note that Content Credentials do not provide value judgments about whether a given set of provenance data is 'true', but instead merely whether the provenance information is well-formed and free from tampering.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"f0f7501e-6cd1-46b5-8a9b-dd6192c0537c","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:38:27.354Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"634f58af-499f-4e4e-a339-524fe61a087e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

| Blaxel Blog\",\"url\":\"https://blaxel.ai/blog/trusted-execution-environment\"},{\"title\":\"Confidential Computing for AI Workloads - by Elsie Jang\",\"url\":\"https://elsiejang1.substack.com/p/confidential-computing-for-ai-workloads\"},{\"title\":\"Anthropic's \\\"Confidential Inference\\\" project and its ...\",\"url\":\"https://www.linkedin.com/posts/sungkun-moon-2395a864_confidential-inference-via-trusted-virtual-activity-7343430703038021633-xrg2\"},{\"title\":\"Confidential Inference via Trusted Virtual Machines | Felix ...\",\"url\":\"https://www.linkedin.com/posts/felixschuster_confidential-inference-via-trusted-virtual-activity-7341154352130105344-J-X2\"}]\n\nI've completed the web search for your query on Anthropic's confidential inference research. Here are the key findings:\n\n## Anthropic's Confidential Inference Announcement\n\nIn June 2025, Anthropic and Pattern Labs (now Irregular) published a whitepaper on confidential inference systems. The guiding principle is that sensitive data should remain encrypted except at the point where it's processed, using a chain of trust that attests to the security of software and enforces rules about which software is allowed to use encryption keys.\n\n## Key Architecture Components\n\nConfidential Inference is a set of tools used to process encrypted data and show that such data is only readable within servers that can prove themselves trustworthy. The system uses a small trusted loader running on a separate virtual machine isolated by the hypervisor that presents itself as a \"virtual accelerator\" and only accepts programs signed by Anthropic's secure continuous integration server.\n\n## Attestation and Security\n\nThe report describes the loader running in a confidential computing environment with features including encrypted memory isolated by hardware from other workloads and cryptographic proof that the correct code is being run. The paper details design principles and security risks for using confidential computing to protect model weights during inference, building on RAND's SL4 and SL5 security levels designed to withstand weight theft attempts.\n\n## Dual Use Cases\n\nThere are two main reasons to adopt these tools: Model Weight Security against increasingly capable threat actors, and User Security to prove that sensitive user data is kept private.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"cc5fb0af-5599-4563-9ece-6d65cc6b2c2b","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:38:29.886Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"49828f19-0841-44a7-a56c-7d39fc163253","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Opentimestamps.org - Blockchain Developer Tools & Resources (2026)\",\"url\":\"https://cryptolinks.com/756/opentimestamps\"},{\"title\":\"Building Tamper-Evident Audit Trails for Trading Systems: A VCP v1.1 Implementation Guide - DEV Community\",\"url\":\"https://dev.to/veritaschain/building-tamper-evident-audit-trails-for-trading-systems-a-vcp-v11-implementation-guide-3b2d\"},{\"title\":\"OpenTimestamps — Grokipedia\",\"url\":\"https://grokipedia.com/page/opentimestamps\"},{\"title\":\"snaproot: Decentralized File Integrity Verification Using Blockchain-Anchored Cryptographic Hashing\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.10625\"},{\"title\":\"How OpenTimestamps Bitcoin Anchoring Works: SHA-256 Hashing & Blockchain Timestamping Explained | ProofSnap\",\"url\":\"https://getproofsnap.com/posts/blockchain-timestamping.html\"},{\"title\":\"Notarial timestamps savings in logs management via Merkle trees and Key   Derivation Functions\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2110.02103\"},{\"title\":\"Blockchain Timestamping Guide: Securing Digital Proof\",\"url\":\"https://originstamp.com/en/blog/reader/blockchain-timestamp\"},{\"title\":\"Building Cryptographic Audit Trails for TradingView Strategies with VCP - DEV Community\",\"url\":\"https://dev.to/veritaschain/building-cryptographic-audit-trails-for-tradingview-strategies-with-vcp-44ka\"}]\n\nI've completed the web search on OpenTimestamps and related concepts. Here's what I found:\n\n## Key Findings\n\n**OpenTimestamps Protocol:**\nOpenTimestamps is an open-source timestamping protocol that leverages the Bitcoin blockchain to create and independently verify proofs of a document's or data's existence prior to a specific point in time, without relying on centralized trusted authorities. Developed by Bitcoin contributor Peter Todd, it provides a standardized, permissionless format for aggregating multiple timestamps into efficient Merkle trees, enabling scalable and cost-effective notarization through a single blockchain transaction.\n\n**Merkle Tree Aggregation:**\nThe protocol is for anchoring timestamps in a Bitcoin network. It creates a Merkle tree of multiple document hashes and anchors only the root hash to the blockchain, which makes it cost-efficient.\n\n**Tamper-Evidence:**\nBy anchoring hashes to Bitcoin's distributed ledger, OTS offers a tamper-evident record that cannot be altered retroactively, addressing the need for trustworthy temporal proofs in digital environments.\n\n**Comparison to RFC 3161:**\nIf you've used traditional timestamp authorities (e.g., RFC 3161 servers), think of OpenTimestamps as the same core goal—proving time—but without needing a central authority. Traditional Time-Stamping Authorities provide reliable temporal evidence. However, they operate as single points of trust and do not supply a tamper-evident record of event ordering.\n\n**Proof Format:**\nAn OpenTimestamps proof is a compact .ots file containing the Merkle path connecting your document's hash to a specific Bitcoin block header. Verification requires no trusted third party — anyone with a Bitcoin node can independently confirm the proof.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"9cea20f1-6c4e-4b41-9511-a78baf90063a","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:39:05.142Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6fd53271-eb13-4256-951f-d12257aca9b4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Keyless Signing Explained\",\"url\":\"https://safeguard.sh/resources/blog/what-is-sigstore\"},{\"title\":\"Keyless Signing | sigstore/cosign | DeepWiki\",\"url\":\"https://deepwiki.com/sigstore/cosign/4.1-keyless-signing\"},{\"title\":\"Keyless Signing with Sigstore: Simplifying Artifact Security | OpsCanary\",\"url\":\"https://opscanary.com/articles/keyless-signing-with-sigstore-simplifying-artifact-security-4ba04a1c\"},{\"title\":\"Securing Artifacts: Keyless Signing with Sigstore and CI/MON - Cycode\",\"url\":\"https://cycode.com/blog/securing-artifacts-keyless-signing-with-sigstore-and-ci-mon/\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's comprehensive information about OpenPubkey, Sigstore keyless signing, OIDC identity, Fulcio, and ephemeral keys:\n\n## Overview of Keyless Signing\n\nKeyless signing associates identities, rather than keys, with an artifact signature. Sigstore uses \"keyless signing,\" where a developer's identity — not a stored private key — becomes the root of trust for a signature.\n\n## How the System Works\n\nThe keyless signing process involves several key components:\n\n**Ephemeral Keys**: When a developer or CI job runs cosign sign, the tool generates a brand-new ephemeral key pair in memory, then authenticates to Fulcio (Sigstore's certificate authority) using an OpenID Connect (OIDC) token from a provider like GitHub Actions, Google, or Microsoft.\n\n**Fulcio and Certificate Issuance**: Fulcio issues short-lived certificates binding an ephemeral key to an OpenID Connect identity. Fulcio verifies that OIDC token and issues a short-lived X.509 certificate — typically valid for around 10 to 20 minutes — that binds the ephemeral public key to the verified identity, such as a specific GitHub Actions workflow file and repository.\n\n**Transparency Logging**: Signing events are logged in Rekor, a signature transparency log, providing an auditable record of when a signature was created.\n\n## OpenPubkey vs. Sigstore\n\nThe Sigstore blog provides a detailed comparison of OpenPubkey with Sigstore's architecture. In OpenPubKey, the user goes through a similar, albeit simpler flow: A user logs into their identity provider (Google, Github, Microsoft, etc.). However, relying directly on OIDC signing keys for verification introduces a large amount of complexity (and attack surface) on clients, who have to handle the key rotation aspects of OIDC providers.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"f0538462-266d-448a-9492-72b68292aa97","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:39:06.209Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"401d475b-bb53-4e3f-b451-1173a0dd3e0b","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

- Lagrange-Labs/deep-prove: Framework to prove inference of ML models blazingly fast\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/Lagrange-Labs/deep-prove\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, here's information about DeepProve by Lagrange in the context of zkML benchmarks and verifiable inference:\n\n## Performance Benchmarks\n\nDeepProve generates LLM inference proofs up to 60x faster than the prior zkML state-of-the-art, with 671x faster verification. DeepProve preserves model accuracy.\n\n## Verifiable Inference & Production Use\n\nDeepProve is a zero-knowledge machine-learning system that creates a cryptographic proof for an AI inference—a receipt that says the right model ran on the right input and produced the output you're looking at. The verifier can check that proof in milliseconds, without needing access to private model weights or input data.\n\nDeepProve is the first production-ready zkML system to successfully generate a cryptographic proof of a full large language model (LLM) inference. A zero-knowledge proof was successfully generated for the full inference of OpenAI's GPT-2.\n\n## Production Deployment\n\nDeepProve has operated in production for twelve months since the first full-LLM proof was generated in August 2025. DeepProve served as the proof layer behind Turing Roulette, a live public demonstration in which more than 500,000 unique participants generated 3.7 million live AI inferences, each cryptographically verified.\n\nThe system is now open-source and available at https://github.com/Lagrange-Labs/deep-prove.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"0fe5effa-1166-4be9-9dac-cc3396509cd1","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:39:06.263Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"720c50c7-8287-4708-a1c2-71e28c652a67","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

github.com/transparency-dev/armored-witness/cmd/verify_build - Go Packages\",\"url\":\"https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/transparency-dev/armored-witness/cmd/verify_build\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - transparency-dev/armored-witness · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/transparency-dev/armored-witness\"},{\"title\":\"Can I Get A Witness (Network)?\",\"url\":\"https://blog.transparency.dev/can-i-get-a-witness-network\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - transparency-dev/witness: Libraries and binaries for running witnesses for verifiable logs · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/transparency-dev/witness\"},{\"title\":\"From Witnessing to Transparent Ecosystems | Trillian\",\"url\":\"https://transparency.dev/summit2024/witness.html\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - transparency-dev/armored-witness-os · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/transparency-dev/armored-witness-os\"},{\"title\":\"github.com/transparency-dev/armored-witness-boot | Go | ...\",\"url\":\"https://deps.dev/go/github.com/transparency-dev/armored-witness-boot/v0.1.0\"},{\"title\":\"Autonomous exchange via entrusted ledger immutable distributed database\",\"url\":\"https://image-ppubs.uspto.gov/dirsearch-public/print/downloadPdf/11126613\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, here's what I found about transparency.dev's Armored Witness and distributed witness network:\n\n## Armored Witness Hardware\n\nThe ArmoredWitness device is a small networked device based on the USB armory, adding an RJ45 LAN port which supports PoE, and running an open-source implementation of a witness configured to support a growing number of transparency-enabled ecosystems. The new device is an open source hardware design.\n\nThe ArmoredWitness was engineered to be fully transparent, open to inspection and verification.\n\n## Witness Network Purpose\n\nA witness is an entity that implements a gossip protocol to effectively verify that logs are append-only, ensuring their integrity and preventing unauthorized modifications. The witness is an HTTP service that stores checkpoints it has seen from different verifiable logs in a sqlite database. This is a very lightweight way to help detect or even prevent split-view attacks.\n\n## Split View Protection\n\nThe distributed nature of Armored Witness devices ensures that split views of the log are prevented due to the geographically distributed countersigning by the witness network.\n\n## Distribution Plans\n\nA small number of these devices will be distributed to folks who are passionate about one or more of the witnessed ecosystems. Once provisioned, the ArmoredWitness devices will only run the ArmoredWitness firmware, so will not be repurposable for other use cases.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"a786dfd2-7cc6-435b-bd07-8ec74626ff9c","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:39:06.593Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ec6963ae-453b-4a42-941d-5f4928d81a97","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
