{"parentUuid":"ab58c3e3-7222-444c-9311-196488460c11","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"7ce8881e-5fa8-4b31-a62a-9ff4d72be4e8","agentId":"a8f210f6f7381fe6e","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015ZmacF13kUVYeYrvwAesaw","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"proof-carrying data proof-carrying code provenance verifiable computation lineage concept\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Building Verifiable Computations - EZKL Blog\",\"url\":\"https://blog.ezkl.xyz/post/pcd/\"},{\"title\":\"SNARKs to the rescue: proof-of-contact in zero knowledge\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2005.12676\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - arkworks-rs/pcd: Implementations of proof-carrying data · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/arkworks-rs/pcd\"},{\"title\":\"Proof-Carrying Data and Hearsay Arguments from Signature Cards\",\"url\":\"https://projects.csail.mit.edu/pcd/\"},{\"title\":\"Privacy-preserving contact tracing\",\"url\":\"https://image-ppubs.uspto.gov/dirsearch-public/print/downloadPdf/12001584\"},{\"title\":\"Geospatial-temporal pathogen tracing in zero knowledge\",\"url\":\"https://image-ppubs.uspto.gov/dirsearch-public/print/downloadPdf/12457110\"},{\"title\":\"Proof-carrying data\",\"url\":\"https://dspace.mit.edu/handle/1721.1/61151\"},{\"title\":\"Proof-Carrying Data and Hearsay Arguments from Signature Cards\",\"url\":\"https://conference.iiis.tsinghua.edu.cn/ICS2010/content/paper/Paper_25.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"Breaking the $O(\\\\sqrt n)$-Bit Barrier: Byzantine Agreement with Polylog   Bits Per Party\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2002.02516\"},{\"title\":\"A formally verified compiler back-end\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/0902.2137\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's an overview of these interconnected concepts:\n\n## Proof-Carrying Data (PCD)\n\nProof-carrying data is a method where data itself carries a proof of some computation. More formally, PCD is a cryptographic primitive that allows the incremental verification of a distributed computation that can continue indefinitely, where a computation defined by a directed acyclic graph is augmented by attaching a succinct proof of correctness to each message, allowing any intermediate state of the computation to be verified efficiently.\n\nIn PCD, the system designer prescribes the desired properties of a computation's outputs. Corresponding proofs are attached to every message flowing through the system, and are mutually verified by the system's components. Each such proof attests that the message's data and all of its history comply with the prescribed properties.\n\n## Verifiable Computation\n\nVerifiable computation is the notion of proving correctness and security between nodes in network systems, and PCD is an approach to achieving verifiable computation through cryptographic mechanisms.\n\n## Proof-Carrying Code (PCC)\n\nProof-carrying code focuses on the generation of independently-checkable evidence that compiled code satisfies a behavioral specification such as type and memory safety. PCC makes use of a certifying compiler, which either fails or returns both compiled code and a proof of the property.\n\n## Relationship to Lineage and Provenance\n\nThe search results show these concepts are closely related to data provenance and lineage tracking in distributed systems, particularly in ensuring that data history and computation chains maintain compliance with specified security properties throughout their entire execution path.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"77c8f150-b939-42ff-a5d8-a52431b956d3","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:39:31.977Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ab58c3e3-7222-444c-9311-196488460c11","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"1a4471ee-1939-4cc7-8460-3bb7015dff4a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"7ce8881e-5fa8-4b31-a62a-9ff4d72be4e8","agentId":"a8f210f6f7381fe6e","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018EbeE7eey3jXNdtKVsVeAM","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"in-toto attestation predicate SLSA provenance v1 schema subject statement DSSE envelope\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Artifact Provenance and Attestations: From SLSA to in-toto | Secure Pipelines\",\"url\":\"https://secure-pipelines.com/ci-cd-security/artifact-provenance-attestations-slsa-in-toto/\"},{\"title\":\"SLSA Provenance Blog Series, Part 1: What Is Software Attestation\",\"url\":\"https://www.legitsecurity.com/blog/slsa-provenance-blog-series-part-1-what-is-software-attestation\"},{\"title\":\"SLSA Build Provenance: Verifying Supply Chain Integrity from Source to Deployment\",\"url\":\"https://www.systemshardening.com/articles/cicd/slsa-build-provenance/\"},{\"title\":\"attestation/spec/v1/envelope.md at main · in-toto/attestation\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/in-toto/attestation/blob/main/spec/v1/envelope.md\"},{\"title\":\"Human-Certified Module Repositories for the AI Age\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.02512\"},{\"title\":\"Sigstore Bundle Format - Sigstore\",\"url\":\"https://docs.sigstore.dev/about/bundle/\"},{\"title\":\"SLSA • in-toto and SLSA\",\"url\":\"https://slsa.dev/blog/2023/05/in-toto-and-slsa\"},{\"title\":\"Kettle: Attested builds for verifiable software provenance\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.08363\"},{\"title\":\"AEX: Non-Intrusive Multi-Hop Attestation and Provenance for LLM APIs\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.14283\"}]\n\nI've found comprehensive information about in-toto attestations, SLSA provenance, and DSSE envelopes. Here's what the search results reveal:\n\n## Core Structure\n\nAn in-toto attestation has two layers: the DSSE envelope and the statement. The specification for in-toto attestations can be broken down to three layers: The DSSE Envelope (\"Dead Simple Signing Envelope\"): the transport layer. The in-toto Statement: the attestation header. The predicate: the attestation payload.\n\n## DSSE Envelope\n\nThe DSSE envelope carries the statement of the attestation and provides the cryptographic context needed to verify the authenticity of the attestation. Dead Simple Signing Envelope (DSSE) is a lightweight container format for signed payloads, standardized at github.com/secure-systems-lab/dsse.\n\n## Statement and Subject\n\nThe Statement is the outer envelope that binds a predicate to one or more subjects. The subject consists of one or more artifact references, each identified by a name and a set of cryptographic digests. This binds the predicate to specific artifacts.\n\n## SLSA Provenance v1\n\nSLSA provenance is an in-toto predicate type. When a SLSA-compliant build platform generates provenance, it produces an in-toto attestation with predicateType: https://slsa.dev/provenance/v1, signs it with DSSE, and associates it with the built artifact via the subject field. The predicateType URI selects the schema for the predicate object.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"8ea621f4-72c6-4eb7-8886-44c80f519686","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:39:33.479Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"1a4471ee-1939-4cc7-8460-3bb7015dff4a","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"75c40e36-b3aa-4d1f-a942-cb27539cf1b3","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"7ce8881e-5fa8-4b31-a62a-9ff4d72be4e8","agentId":"a8f210f6f7381fe6e","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_011K5k8dPc7Rrvv6fgM6TkLc","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"Shamir secret sharing social recovery threshold approval destructive action governance multisig dead man switch\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Introduction to Shamir’s Secret Sharing - How to share a secret | AccessDenied\",\"url\":\"https://rya-sge.github.io/access-denied/2024/10/29/shamir-secret-sharing-introduction/\"},{\"title\":\"Shamir Secret Sharing vs Multisig: Which Backup Method ...\",\"url\":\"https://ryder.id/blogs/post/shamir-secret-sharing-vs-multisig-which-backup-method-better-protects-your-crypto\"},{\"title\":\"Multisig, Shamir's Secret Sharing, & MPC Compared\",\"url\":\"https://bitcoinmagazine.com/technical/multisig-shamirs-secret-sharing-mpc-compared\"},{\"title\":\"Multisig, Shamir's secret sharing, & MPC compared - Unchained\",\"url\":\"https://www.unchained.com/blog/mpc-vs-multisig-vs-sss\"},{\"title\":\"Shamir's Secret Sharing shortcomings\",\"url\":\"https://blog.casa.io/shamirs-secret-sharing-security-shortcomings/\"},{\"title\":\"Condition-Triggered Cryptographic Asset Control via Dormant Authorization Paths\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.07933\"},{\"title\":\"Shamir's Secret Sharing ∞ Guide\",\"url\":\"https://encrypthos.com/guide/shamirs-secret-sharing/\"}]\n\nI've completed a web search for your query on Shamir secret sharing and related governance and security mechanisms. Here are the key findings:\n\n## Shamir Secret Sharing Overview\n\nShamir Secret Sharing is a cryptographic method that splits a single secret into multiple shares, with a threshold required to reconstruct. A 2-of-3 setup produces three shares; any two recover the master secret.\n\n## Governance and Threshold Approval\n\nThis setup aligns well with governance policies, such as requiring approvals from multiple executives to initiate a transaction. A threshold requirement describes a structure that involves multiple, separately secured components, where a subset of those components are needed to approve any withdrawal. This is the only way of achieving institutional-grade security, with single points of failure eliminated completely.\n\n## Multisig vs. Shamir\n\nMultisig is the better tool when multiple parties are involved in custody, when you want every transaction to require coordination, and when you can absorb the operational overhead of running multi-party signing. Treasuries and DAOs are the obvious case. Every disbursement requires the configured threshold of signers.\n\n## Social Recovery Applications\n\nShamir's secret sharing enables (t, n)-threshold reconstruction of a master secret. Social recovery wallets apply this principle by designating guardians who can collectively recover access.\n\n## Implementation Considerations\n\nShamir's Secret Sharing suffers from a property where they are just complex enough that people are excited to implement them often for little good reason, and then they are complex enough they implement them poorly.\n\nThe search results provide technical details on these mechanisms but don't contain specific information about \"dead man switch\" implementations with these technologies.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"c1d35305-483e-4a69-b5d0-344d0107c0bb","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:39:34.341Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"75c40e36-b3aa-4d1f-a942-cb27539cf1b3","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"5af7241e-2e2c-49b6-8500-22c7616ac81f","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"7ce8881e-5fa8-4b31-a62a-9ff4d72be4e8","agentId":"a8f210f6f7381fe6e","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NhunZwKuhUrQ39Hbzj7AbS","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"Merkle mountain range compact range append-only log consistency proof prune head\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Merkle Mountain Range (MMR)\",\"url\":\"https://docs.grin.mw/wiki/chain-state/merkle-mountain-range/\"},{\"title\":\"Merkle Mountain Ranges are Optimal: On Witness Update Frequency for Cryptographic Accumulators | Advances in Cryptology – CRYPTO 2025\",\"url\":\"https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1007/978-3-032-01878-6_6\"},{\"title\":\"draft-bryce-cose-merkle-mountain-range-proofs-00 - Datatracker\",\"url\":\"https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-bryce-cose-merkle-mountain-range-proofs-00\"},{\"title\":\"commonware > Merkle Mountain Ranges for Performant Data Authentication\",\"url\":\"https://commonware.xyz/blogs/mmr\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - zmitton/merkle-mountain-range: Merkle tree optimized for lists of sequentially appended data and its proofs · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/zmitton/merkle-mountain-range\"},{\"title\":\"Merkle Mountain Ranges are optimal - Cryptology ePrint Archive\",\"url\":\"https://eprint.iacr.org/2025/234.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"Merkle Tree Certificate Post-Quantum PKI for Kubernetes and Cloud-Native 5G/B5G Core\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.04191\"},{\"title\":\"The Merkle Mountain Belt\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2511.13582v1\"}]\n\nI've performed the web search on Merkle mountain ranges, compact ranges, append-only logs, consistency proofs, and pruning. Here are the key findings:\n\n## Core Concepts\n\nA Merkle Mountain Range (MMR) is strictly append-only: elements are added from the left to the right, adding a parent as soon as 2 children exist, filling up the range accordingly.\n\nA MMR is a list of perfect binary trees, called mountains, each of strictly decreasing height. The leaves of the MMR store the (positioned) hash of each element in the order of their insertion.\n\n## Performance Benefits\n\nWhen adding an element to the MMR, very little data needs to be read in from storage (typically cached), and new data generated by the addition can be persisted to storage with one contiguous write. This makes for a minimal amount of total hash computations (~2n), while maintaining the property that no nodes are updated when appending new leaves (nodes are merely added).\n\n## Consistency Proofs\n\nA consistency proof shows that the accumulator for tree-size-1 is a prefix of the accumulator for tree-size-2. The signature is over the complete accumulator for tree-size-2 obtained using the proof and the, supplied, possibly empty, list of right-peaks which complete the accumulator for tree-size-2.\n\n## Compact Ranges\n\nThe search results mention that Google's Transparency team proposed a similar concept, compact ranges, though specific technical details about compact ranges are limited in these results.\n\nThe research also shows that a close variant of the Merkle Mountain range is essentially optimal for append-only set commitments with efficient updates.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"68b628ad-544b-4186-9fc7-7ce341f88333","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:39:34.404Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"5af7241e-2e2c-49b6-8500-22c7616ac81f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"3d513b85-d060-4223-a12d-4b5b3b02a3f9","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"7ce8881e-5fa8-4b31-a62a-9ff4d72be4e8","agentId":"a8f210f6f7381fe6e","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01R5NbcqNAKDZxDEdhZSawDK","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"OpenVEX VEX vulnerability exploitability exchange attestation signed security finding verifiable credential 2024\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"GitHub - hahwul/vex.cr: OpenVEX (Vulnerability Exploitability eXchange) implementation for Crystal · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/hahwul/vex.cr\"},{\"title\":\"Toward greater transparency: Introducing Machine-readable Vulnerability Exploitability Xchange (VEX) for Azure Linux and beyond\",\"url\":\"https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/msrc/blog/2025/10/toward-greater-transparency-machine-readable-vulnerability-exploitability-xchange-for-azure-linux\"},{\"title\":\"Vulnerability Exploitability eXchange (VEX) beta files now available\",\"url\":\"https://www.redhat.com/en/blog/vulnerability-exploitability-exchange-vex-beta-files-now-available\"},{\"title\":\"VEX - Vulnerability Exploitability eXchange — Kusari\",\"url\":\"https://www.kusari.dev/learning-center/vex---vulnerability-exploitability-exchange\"},{\"title\":\"What Is VEX(Vulnerability Exploitability eXchange)?\",\"url\":\"https://www.aquasec.com/cloud-native-academy/vulnerability-management/vulnerability-exploitability-exchange/\"},{\"title\":\"S3C2 Summit 2024-08: Government Secure Supply Chain Summit\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.00924\"},{\"title\":\"OpenVEX – Open Source Security Foundation\",\"url\":\"https://openssf.org/projects/openvex/\"},{\"title\":\"FLARE-AI: Flaw Reporting for AI\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.31567\"},{\"title\":\"Vulnerability Exploitability eXchange (VEX) | CycloneDX\",\"url\":\"https://cyclonedx.org/capabilities/vex/\"},{\"title\":\"Vulnerability Exploitability eXchange (VEX)\",\"url\":\"https://docs.checkmarx.com/en/34965-543091-vulnerability-exploitability-exchange--vex-.html\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about OpenVEX and VEX:\n\n## Overview\n\nOpenVEX is an implementation of the Vulnerability Exploitability Exchange (VEX for short) that is designed to be minimal, compliant, interoperable, and embeddable.\n\n## What is VEX?\n\nVEX is a rapidly maturing industry standard for communicating the exploitability status of vulnerabilities across complex software ecosystems. Designed to be both machine-readable and human actionable, VEX enables organizations to quickly assess whether a given vulnerability affects specific products, without wading through noise or ambiguity.\n\nA Vulnerability Exploitability eXchange (VEX) is a 'companion artifact' and is a form of a security advisory that indicates whether a product is affected by a known vulnerability or vulnerabilities, thus supporting more effective use of SBOMs.\n\n## Format and Implementation\n\nOpenVEX documents are minimal JSON-LD files that capture the minimal requirements for VEX as defined by the VEX working group. The most common implementations include OpenVEX, CycloneDX VEX, and CSAF VEX (Common Security Advisory Framework).\n\n## Use Cases\n\nVEX is a machine-readable way for software producers to assert whether a given product is affected by a known vulnerability, so consumers can avoid chasing false positives from SBOM-based vulnerability scanners.\n\nMicrosoft is now publishing standard attestations about third-party CVEs through the Vulnerability Exploitability eXchange (VEX) standard including vulnerabilities in embedded open-source software in Microsoft products and services and starting with the Azure Linux Distribution (formerly CBL-Mariner).\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"95648c25-a5fe-46cd-83d4-84a620f8ce6a","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:39:36.529Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3d513b85-d060-4223-a12d-4b5b3b02a3f9","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

WebFetch is fully blocked in this environment, so everything below is synthesized from WebSearch results with primary-source URLs. Here is the report.\n\n---\n\n# Cryptographic Provenance / Transparency + Sovereign / Confidential AI (2023–2026)\n## Deep research for the SIGIL signed-spine / PCF-cert / WARDEN architecture\n\nA note on your existing primitives, so the mapping is precise: your **signed-spine** (Ed25519, hash-chained, Merkle-prune head) is already a *private* tamper-evident log; the entire thrust of the state of the art is turning a private hash chain into a *publicly verifiable, split-view-resistant transparency log* and turning each finding into a *standardized, offline-verifiable attestation*. Nearly every technique below is an \"upgrade path\" for exactly those two moves, plus a hardware/root-of-trust story (TEEs) and a governance story (threshold).\n\n---\n\n## 1. Transparency logs / verifiable append-only logs\n\n**The core idea you're missing today:** a hash chain proves *internal* consistency to whoever holds it, but it does not stop a **split-view attack** — you showing auditor A one log and auditor B a different, equally-well-signed log. The transparency-log ecosystem solves this with three additions on top of a Merkle log: **signed checkpoints**, **inclusion + consistency proofs**, and a **witness/gossip network**.\n\n**Techniques & standards**\n- **Certificate Transparency, RFC 6962** — the foundational Merkle-tree log. A **Signed Tree Head (STH)** commits to the whole log; **inclusion proofs** show an entry is in the tree; **consistency proofs** show tree size N is an append-only prefix of size N+k. Split-view is caught by **gossiping STHs** between clients/auditors. ([rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6962.html](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6962.html))\n- **Sigstore / Rekor** — a signature transparency log for software. Rekor returns an **inclusion proof on upload**, which you persist next to the artifact. **Rekor v2** (GA) moved the backend to a **tile-based** log (Tessera) to cut operating cost, and is designed for **witnessing** (async: clients ask witnesses to check consistency; sync: log asks witnesses to sign every checkpoint). Notably, Rekor v2 *launches without witnessing* pending a public witness network — a candid signal of ecosystem maturity. ([github.com/sigstore/rekor](https://github.com/sigstore/rekor), [blog.sigstore.dev/rekor-v2-ga](https://blog.sigstore.dev/rekor-v2-ga/), [blog.sigstore.dev/using-rekor-monitor](https://blog.sigstore.dev/using-rekor-monitor/))\n- **Trillian → Trillian Tessera** — Google's verifiable-log library; Tessera is the next-gen **tile-based** implementation (static, cacheable Merkle tiles served over a `tlog-tiles` API), the same design proven at scale by the **Go module checksum database** (`sum.golang.org`) and Pixel binary transparency. Tiles make a log cheap to run *and cheap for anyone to mirror/witness*. ([blog.transparency.dev/introducing-trillian-tessera](https://blog.transparency.dev/introducing-trillian-tessera), [transparency.dev/articles/tile-based-logs](https://transparency.dev/articles/tile-based-logs/), [go.dev/blog/module-mirror-launch](https://go.dev/blog/module-mirror-launch))\n- **Checkpoint / witness / gossip model (C2SP)** — the modern, log-agnostic spec set:\n  - **C2SP checkpoint / signed-note** format: a signed-note over `origin + tree-size + root-hash`.\n  - **`tlog-witness`** spec: a witness is an HTTP service that stores checkpoints in SQLite and **countersigns** a new checkpoint only if it carries a valid **RFC 6962 consistency proof** from the last checkpoint it witnessed. A **quorum of witnesses** = split-view resistance, because a malicious log would have to fork *all* witnesses. ([github.com/C2SP/C2SP/blob/main/tlog-witness.md](https://github.com/C2SP/C2SP/blob/main/tlog-witness.md), [github.com/transparency-dev/witness](https://github.com/transparency-dev/witness), [blog.transparency.dev/can-i-get-a-witness-network](https://blog.transparency.dev/can-i-get-a-witness-network))\n  - **Armored Witness** — open-source *hardware* witness (USB-armory + PoE Ethernet) distributed to independent operators for geographically diverse countersigning. ([github.com/transparency-dev/armored-witness](https://github.com/transparency-dev/armored-witness), [transparency.dev/summit2024/witness.html](https://transparency.dev/summit2024/witness.html))\n- **Key Transparency (KT)** — CT applied to *public keys*, using a **verifiable log-backed map** (not just a log) plus **VRFs** to hide user identifiers. Deployed: **WhatsApp Auditable Key Directory (AKD)**, **Apple iMessage Contact Key Verification**, and **Cloudflare's third-party KT auditor** for WhatsApp. Relevant to you because it solves \"how does a verifier know they have the *right* signing key\" — the missing half of any Ed25519 story. ([security.apple.com/blog/imessage-contact-key-verification](https://security.apple.com/blog/imessage-contact-key-verification/), [developers.cloudflare.com/key-transparency](https://developers.cloudflare.com/key-transparency/), [iacr.org/submit/files/slides/2024/rwc/.../18/slides.pdf](https://iacr.org/submit/files/slides/2024/rwc/rwc2024/18/slides.pdf))\n- **Merkle Mountain Range (MMR) / compact ranges** — the append-optimal accumulator that maps directly onto your **Merkle-prune head**: leaves only added (never updated), ~2n hashes, single contiguous write, and consistency proofs are \"old peaks are a prefix of new peaks.\" A CRYPTO 2025 result proves MMRs are essentially optimal for append-only accumulators; there's even an IETF draft for **COSE MMR proofs**. This is the right internal data structure to keep while adding checkpoints. ([eprint.iacr.org/2025/234.pdf](https://eprint.iacr.org/2025/234.pdf), [commonware.xyz/blogs/mmr](https://commonware.xyz/blogs/mmr), [datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-bryce-cose-merkle-mountain-range-proofs-00](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-bryce-cose-merkle-mountain-range-proofs-00))\n\n**Maturity:** CT/RFC 6962 and Go sumdb are production-grade and battle-tested. Tessera/tlog-tiles/`tlog-witness` are GA-to-late-beta (2024–2025). Public **witness networks** are the least-mature link — still being stood up — which is actually an opportunity (below).\n\n**How it plugs into the signed-spine:**\n1. Keep the Ed25519 hash chain, but re-express the head as an **MMR/compact-range** and periodically emit a **C2SP signed-note checkpoint** over `(origin=\"vigil-sigil\", tree-size, root)`.\n2. Serve the log as **tiles** so any auditor can pull it incrementally and recompute inclusion/consistency proofs.\n3. Stand up **N witnesses** (yours + independent third parties/customers) that countersign checkpoints under `tlog-witness` → your evidence log becomes **split-view-resistant**, i.e. you can no longer show two clients divergent finding histories.\n4. Emit an **inclusion proof + witnessed checkpoint alongside every PCF-cert** so a finding is verifiable in isolation.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Supply-chain provenance (the model for a \"proof-carrying finding\")\n\n**Techniques & standards**\n- **in-toto Attestation Framework** (CNCF) — the generic container for \"X did Y to artifact Z.\" Three layers: **DSSE envelope** (Dead Simple Signing Envelope, the signed transport) → **Statement** (binds a `subject` = artifact name + cryptographic digests) → **predicate** (typed payload, selected by a `predicateType` URI). ([in-toto.io](https://in-toto.io), [github.com/in-toto/attestation](https://github.com/in-toto/attestation/blob/main/spec/v1/envelope.md))\n- **SLSA** (Supply-chain Levels for Software Artifacts) — the *opinionated* layer on top of in-toto: `predicateType: https://slsa.dev/provenance/v1` defines exactly what a build must attest, and SLSA levels (L1–L3) grade the strength of the provenance. ([slsa.dev](https://slsa.dev), [slsa.dev/spec/v1.1/faq](https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.1/faq), [slsa.dev/blog/2023/05/in-toto-and-slsa](https://slsa.dev/blog/2023/05/in-toto-and-slsa))\n- **SCITT — Supply Chain Integrity, Transparency and Trust (IETF)** — *the* standard to model your PCF-cert on. Flow: an issuer creates a **Signed Statement** (COSE_Sign1) about any subject → a **Transparency Service** checks a **Registration Policy** and appends it to an append-only (Merkle) ledger → returns a **Receipt** = a signed, **offline-verifiable inclusion proof** → the statement + receipt = a **Transparent Statement**. It is deliberately domain-agnostic (any single-issuer signed content, not just software). ([scitt.io](https://scitt.io), [datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-scitt-architecture](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-scitt-architecture/), [conserver.io/deep-dives/scitt](https://www.conserver.io/deep-dives/scitt-supply-chain-integrity-transparency-and-trust))\n- **OpenVEX / VEX** — signed, machine-readable **security findings** (\"product P is / isn't affected by CVE-C, status, justification\"). This is the closest existing *security-domain* attestation to a finding. OpenVEX is minimal JSON-LD; CycloneDX-VEX and CSAF are alternatives. Microsoft, Red Hat now publish signed VEX. ([openssf.org/projects/openvex](https://openssf.org/projects/openvex/), [cyclonedx.org/capabilities/vex](https://cyclonedx.org/capabilities/vex/))\n- **C2PA / Content Credentials** — provenance for *media and now text*. A **Manifest** holds signed **assertions** (origin, edits, and **`digitalSourceType`** marking AI/ML authorship). v2.3+ (Dec 2025) adds **manifests for unstructured text** (embedded via non-rendering Unicode variation selectors) and live-stream support; NSA/CISA endorse it. Important caveat the spec states plainly: it proves the provenance record is well-formed and untampered, **not** that the claims are true. ([spec.c2pa.org](https://spec.c2pa.org), [en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Content_Credentials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Content_Credentials), [media.defense.gov/.../CSI-CONTENT-CREDENTIALS.PDF](https://media.defense.gov/2025/Jan/29/2003634788/-1/-1/0/CSI-CONTENT-CREDENTIALS.PDF))\n- **Proof-Carrying Data / Proof-Carrying Code (the intellectual lineage of \"PCF\")** — PCD (MIT) attaches a succinct proof of correctness to every message in a distributed computation, so any intermediate state is verifiable and its *entire history* provably complied with prescribed properties. This is literally the theory behind naming a finding \"proof-carrying.\" ([projects.csail.mit.edu/pcd](https://projects.csail.mit.edu/pcd/), [github.com/arkworks-rs/pcd](https://github.com/arkworks-rs/pcd), [blog.ezkl.xyz/post/pcd](https://blog.ezkl.xyz/post/pcd/))\n\n**Maturity:** in-toto/DSSE/SLSA are production-standard. SCITT is a maturing IETF draft (v22) with running reference implementations. OpenVEX is production. C2PA text manifests are new (2025) but backed by Adobe/Microsoft/OpenAI/BBC.\n\n**How it plugs into PCF-cert:** Redefine a **PCF-cert as a SCITT Signed Statement**: a **COSE_Sign1** (or DSSE) envelope whose `subject` digests the target + evidence bundle (request/response, oracle transcript, screenshot), carrying a **custom `predicateType`** (e.g. `https://vigil.dev/pcf/v1`) whose schema borrows OpenVEX's status/justification vocabulary. Register it in your Transparency Service (your witnessed spine) to get a **Receipt**. Result: a finding that any third party — customer, regulator, court — can verify **offline and forever** for authenticity, contents, issuer identity, and log inclusion. For AI-authored narrative sections, attach a **C2PA manifest** marking `digitalSourceType` = AI.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Confidential computing / TEEs (proving the agent ran unmodified on trusted hardware)\n\n**Techniques & standards**\n- **CPU TEEs:** **Intel TDX** (confidential VMs; strongest attestation, favored for gov/defense/high-security AI), **AMD SEV-SNP** (CVMs, lower overhead), **AWS Nitro Enclaves** (isolated enclave + KMS attestation-gated key release). ([onidel.com/blog/amd-sev-snp-vs-intel-tdx-vps](https://onidel.com/blog/amd-sev-snp-vs-intel-tdx-vps), [redhat.com/.../aws-nitro-enclaves-red-hat-enterprise-linux](https://www.redhat.com/en/blog/deploy-confidential-computing-aws-nitro-enclaves-red-hat-enterprise-linux))\n- **GPU TEEs:** **NVIDIA H100/H200 Confidential Computing** — first GPU TEE, on-die hardware root of trust, encrypted CPU↔GPU bounce buffer, and a **signed GPU attestation report**. **Composite attestation** now spans CPU-TEE + GPU-CC with **1–7% inference overhead**. Blackwell extends this. ([developer.nvidia.com/blog/confidential-computing-on-h100-gpus](https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/confidential-computing-on-h100-gpus-for-secure-and-trustworthy-ai/), [docs.nvidia.com/nvidia-secure-ai-with-blackwell-and-hopper-gpus-whitepaper.pdf](https://docs.nvidia.com/nvidia-secure-ai-with-blackwell-and-hopper-gpus-whitepaper.pdf), [arxiv.org/html/2507.02770v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2507.02770v1))\n- **Remote attestation** = a TEE proves to a remote party that genuine hardware is running expected code (a signed measurement/quote), which then gates **attestation-based key release** — secrets only decrypt if the measurement matches. Runtimes: **Gramine, Occlum, SCONE, Enarx, Fortanix EDP, Nitro SDK**, plus **Red Hat Confidential Containers (CoCo)**. ([debugg.ai/resources/attested-microservices-2025](https://debugg.ai/resources/attested-microservices-2025-confidential-computing-sgx-sev-nitro-enclaves-remote-key-release), [next.redhat.com/2025/10/23/enhancing-ai-inference-security](https://next.redhat.com/2025/10/23/enhancing-ai-inference-security-with-confidential-computing-a-path-to-private-data-inference-with-proprietary-llms/))\n\n**Maturity — with an honest red flag:** production-available on major clouds; overheads now small. **But** the **TEE.Fail attack (ACM CCS 2025)** used a ~$1,000 DDR5 memory-bus interposer to **extract attestation keys from both Intel TDX and AMD SEV-SNP** — physical-access attacks defeat the hardware root of trust. Treat TEE attestation as *raising the bar*, not as unconditional proof; combine with the witnessed log so a compromised-TEE forgery still can't rewrite history. ([search result: \"TEE.Fail attack (ACM CCS 2025)\"](https://unmitigatedrisk.com/?p=1232))\n\n**How it plugs into the architecture:** Run **CRUCIBLE/OBSIDIAN inside a TDX or SEV-SNP CVM** (+ H100-CC if the oracle uses a GPU model). Bind the **attestation quote's measurement hash into each checkpoint and each PCF-cert**, so every finding proves *this exact agent binary ran on genuine hardware*. Use **attestation-gated key release** so the agent's Ed25519 signing key (and offensive credentials) only exist inside the enclave — this is the technical core of \"sovereign agent that can prove it ran unmodified.\"\n\n---\n\n## 4. Verifiable AI inference (proving the LLM actually produced the output)\n\nTwo families, very different maturity:\n\n**(a) zkML — cryptographic proof of inference (trustless, no hardware assumption)**\n- **EZKL** — ONNX → Halo2 zk-SNARK; the widely-used baseline. ([blog.ezkl.xyz](https://blog.ezkl.xyz/post/pcd/))\n- **Lagrange DeepProve** — **production-grade zkML**, open-sourced; **DeepProve-1 generated the first ZK proof of a full LLM (GPT-2) inference (Aug 2025)**, ~60× faster proving and **671× faster verification** than prior SOTA, verification in **milliseconds**, 12M+ proofs generated, and a live demo (Turing Roulette) with 3.7M verified inferences. ([lagrange.dev/blog/deepprove-1](https://lagrange.dev/blog/deepprove-1), [lagrange.dev/blog/deepprove-is-now-open-source](https://lagrange.dev/blog/deepprove-is-now-open-source), [github.com/Lagrange-Labs/deep-prove](https://github.com/Lagrange-Labs/deep-prove))\n- **zkLLM, ZKTorch, ZKML** (ETH/EuroSys), plus a 2025 survey. **Reality check:** for *large* modern LLMs, proving still runs **minutes-to-hours per inference** — not yet practical for a per-finding oracle at frontier model scale. ([arxiv.org/pdf/2502.18535](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.18535), [arxiv.org/pdf/2507.07031](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.07031), [blog.icme.io/the-definitive-guide-to-zkml-2025](https://blog.icme.io/the-definitive-guide-to-zkml-2025/))\n\n**(b) Attested inference — TEE-signed outputs (practical *today*)**\n- **Anthropic \"Confidential Inference via Trusted VMs\" (June 2025, w/ Irregular/Pattern Labs)** — a trusted loader in a CVM presents as a \"virtual accelerator,\" accepts only CI-signed programs, keeps weights/data encrypted except in-use, and proves via attestation which code ran; extends to **prompt privacy + output non-disclosure**. ([anthropic.com/research/confidential-inference-trusted-vms](https://www.anthropic.com/research/confidential-inference-trusted-vms), paper: [assets.anthropic.com/.../Confidential_Inference_Paper.pdf](https://assets.anthropic.com/m/c52125297b85a42/original/Confidential_Inference_Paper.pdf))\n- **Azure Confidential AI Inferencing** — LLM endpoint in a TDX VM with a **publicly verifiable attestation policy** naming the approved model, runtime, and **system-prompt versions**. ([search: Azure Confidential AI Inferencing](https://next.redhat.com/2025/10/23/enhancing-ai-inference-security-with-confidential-computing-a-path-to-private-data-inference-with-proprietary-llms/))\n- Emerging: **bit-exact inference verification** (arxiv 2606.00279), **VeriLLM** (publicly verifiable decentralized inference, arxiv 2509.24257), and multi-hop LLM-API attestation (**AEX**, arxiv 2603.14283).\n\n**Maturity:** attested inference = deployable now (small overhead); zkML = practical for small models and *emerging* for LLMs (DeepProve is the frontier). Signed model outputs (TEE-signed transcripts) are the pragmatic near-term path.\n\n**How it plugs in:** For each **oracle-confirmed finding**, attach a **proof-of-inference receipt**. Near-term: a **TEE-attested inference receipt** (model id + runtime + system-prompt hash, signed inside the enclave) becomes an assertion inside the PCF-cert. Longer-term/high-stakes: a **DeepProve zkML proof** for the specific classifier/oracle step that gates a destructive action, so the *judgment itself* is cryptographically checkable without revealing model weights.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Local-first / sovereign AI (what \"sovereign\" means technically)\n\n**Techniques & concepts**\n- **Local-first software** (Ink & Switch lineage; CRDTs) — data lives on the user's device, works offline, syncs peer-to-peer, and the user retains ultimate ownership/control. Its modern extension is **local-first AI agents**: persistent autonomy + local execution (scheduled jobs, long-term memory, real actions) with no external gatekeeper. ([metapress.com/data-sovereignty-meets-ai-automation](https://metapress.com/data-sovereignty-meets-ai-automation-why-local-first-ai-agents-are-the-future-of-business-operations/))\n- **Sovereign AI — the four pillars** an entity must control: **data sovereignty** (local law, no foreign access), **infrastructure sovereignty** (own hardware), **model sovereignty** (own/open weights), **software sovereignty** (open or self-built tooling, no lock-in). ([redhat.com/en/topics/ai/sovereign-ai](https://www.redhat.com/en/topics/ai/sovereign-ai), [francescogaravaglia.substack.com/p/sovereign-ai-and-local-first-architectures](https://francescogaravaglia.substack.com/p/sovereign-ai-and-local-first-architectures), [enterprisedb.com/what-is-sovereign-ai-data-sovereignty](https://www.enterprisedb.com/what-is-sovereign-ai-data-sovereignty))\n- **Self-hosted stacks:** Ollama / Open WebUI (\"Sovereign AI Platform\"), local vector stores, air-gapped deployment. ([openwebui.com/sovereign-ai](https://openwebui.com/sovereign-ai), [glukhov.org/llm-hosting/self-hosting/](https://www.glukhov.org/llm-hosting/self-hosting/llm-selfhosting-and-ai-sovereignty/))\n- **Policy tailwind:** UK **Sovereign AI Unit** (£500M, July 2025); Canada's **Sovereign AI Compute Strategy** ($2B). Data-privacy is cited by 92% of orgs (Cisco 2025) as a top AI-adoption concern. ([blog.equinix.com/.../designing-for-sovereign-ai](https://blog.equinix.com/blog/2025/10/23/designing-for-sovereign-ai-how-to-keep-data-local-in-a-global-world/), [mirantis.com/blog/sovereign-ai](https://www.mirantis.com/blog/sovereign-ai/))\n\n**How it plugs in:** Your pitch is unusually strong here — a pentest agent runs on *client-owned* infrastructure, against *client-owned* targets, with *client-held* keys. Map your stack to the four pillars explicitly, and note the tension the sovereign-AI literature raises: **autonomy demands control** — the more autonomous the agent, the more the sovereignty story must rest on the *governance* and *audit* layers (§6, §7), not just on \"it runs locally.\" Sovereignty + witnessed transparency is the differentiator: sovereign for privacy, witnessed for accountability.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Threshold cryptography / multi-party control (governing an offensive agent)\n\n**Techniques & standards**\n- **FROST — RFC 9591 (June 2024)** — 2-round threshold Schnorr; efficient m-of-n signing tolerant of unreliable networks. This is the modern default for \"no single key can sign.\" ([rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9591](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9591), [computer.org/csdl/magazine/sp/2024/06/...](https://www.computer.org/csdl/magazine/sp/2024/06/10799187/22EaoD72B7W))\n- **NIST Multi-Party Threshold Cryptography** project — the standardization track (threshold EdDSA/ECDSA, etc.). ([csrc.nist.gov/projects/threshold-cryptography](https://csrc.nist.gov/projects/threshold-cryptography))\n- **MPC threshold signing** in custody (BitGo, Fireblocks) — production-proven m-of-n where no party ever holds the whole key; often combined with **HSM/TEE** for tamper resistance. ([financefeeds.com/multi-party-computation-mpc-threshold-signatures](https://financefeeds.com/multi-party-computation-mpc-threshold-signatures/))\n- **Shamir Secret Sharing vs multisig vs MPC** — SSS reconstructs a secret (single point at reconstruction); **multisig** never reconstructs and requires per-action coordination (better for *governance/approval* of each destructive action); **social recovery** uses guardian shares. ([unchained.com/blog/mpc-vs-multisig-vs-sss](https://www.unchained.com/blog/mpc-vs-multisig-vs-sss), [bitcoinmagazine.com/technical/multisig-shamirs-secret-sharing-mpc-compared](https://bitcoinmagazine.com/technical/multisig-shamirs-secret-sharing-mpc-compared))\n- **Hardware co-signers:** YubiKey / HSM as one of the n shares → a human-held physical factor must participate in high-risk authorizations.\n- **Dead-man's-switch / dormant authorization:** condition-triggered cryptographic control via \"dormant authorization paths\" (arxiv 2603.07933) — formalizes time/condition-gated release, useful as an autonomy circuit-breaker. ([arxiv.org/pdf/2603.07933](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.07933))\n\n**How it plugs into WARDEN:** Gate every **destructive / high-blast-radius action** behind a **FROST m-of-n** (e.g. 2-of-3: the agent's enclave key + a human YubiKey + a policy-HSM). Make the **authorization itself a signed statement written to the witnessed spine**, so \"who approved this exploit and when\" is itself tamper-evident and non-repudiable. Add a **dormant-authorization dead-man's-switch** so autonomy auto-revokes absent a periodic human co-sign. Net property: **no single point — human *or* agent — can authorize destruction**, and every authorization is publicly auditable.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Tamper-evident audit for autonomous agents (prior art)\n\nThe field is young but converging fast on exactly your primitives.\n\n- **Consensus recipe** across practitioners: per-record signing **+** hash chaining **+** external anchoring; capture ≥8 fields per action (full prompt, model-version/config **hash**, tool-call sequence + args, retrieval queries/doc-IDs, output + rationale, human-override events, memory read/writes, cost/latency); store **write-once**. ([aigovernance.com/controls/agent-action-audit-trail](https://aigovernance.com/controls/agent-action-audit-trail), [deepinspect.ai/blog/tamper-evident-audit-logs-for-ai](https://www.deepinspect.ai/blog/tamper-evident-audit-logs-for-ai), [nono.sh/blog/secure-agent-audit](https://nono.sh/blog/secure-agent-audit))\n- **Verifiable execution for agents** — cryptographic proof of what an agent did, incl. SIEM integration requirements. ([aiacceleratorinstitute.com/verifiable-execution-for-ai-agents](https://www.aiacceleratorinstitute.com/verifiable-execution-for-ai-agents/), [kiteworks.com/.../ai-agent-audit-trail-siem-integration](https://www.kiteworks.com/regulatory-compliance/ai-agent-audit-trail-siem-integration/))\n- **Academic frontier (2025–2026):** **VCT** (verifiable transcript system for LLM conversations, arxiv 2606.23003), **\"Trust, but verify\"** (arxiv 2504.13443), **Tool Receipts** for hallucination detection (arxiv 2603.10060), **Proof-of-Guardrail** (arxiv 2603.05786), **AEX** multi-hop LLM-API attestation (arxiv 2603.14283).\n- **Regulatory driver:** **EU AI Act Article 12** (automatic logging for high-risk AI), plus NIST AI RMF, DORA, HIPAA — all demand records an auditor can *rely on as evidence*. ([search: EU AI Act Article 12 logging](https://www.deepinspect.ai/blog/tamper-evident-audit-logs-for-ai))\n\n**How it plugs in:** You are *ahead* of most of this prior art (you already sign + hash-chain). The gap between you and \"state of the art\" is exactly (a) **external anchoring/witnessing** (§1) and (b) **standardized attestations** (§2). Adopting those two is what moves you from \"good internal audit log\" to \"publicly-verifiable accountable autonomy\" — a claim essentially nobody in offensive security can currently make.\n\n---\n\n## The 3–5 highest-leverage additions (world-first territory)\n\nRanked by leverage-to-effort, each cites what to import:\n\n**1. A witnessed, publicly-verifiable transparency log of oracle-confirmed findings.** Re-express the signed-spine as a **tile-based log (Tessera / C2SP `tlog-tiles`)**, emit **C2SP signed-note checkpoints** over the Merkle-prune head, and get them **countersigned by a quorum of independent witnesses (`tlog-witness`, optionally Armored-Witness hardware held by customers)**, then **anchor checkpoints to Bitcoin via OpenTimestamps** for a trustless time-and-existence proof. This yields **split-view resistance + third-party auditability + trustless timestamping** for pentest evidence. No offensive-security product has a witnessed transparency log of confirmed findings — this is genuinely first-of-kind. ([tlog-witness](https://github.com/C2SP/C2SP/blob/main/tlog-witness.md), [Tessera](https://blog.transparency.dev/introducing-trillian-tessera), [Armored Witness](https://github.com/transparency-dev/armored-witness), [OpenTimestamps](https://opentimestamps.org))\n\n**2. PCF-cert as a standards-grade Transparent Statement (SCITT + in-toto/DSSE, with OpenVEX vocabulary).** Make each proof-carrying finding a **COSE_Sign1 Signed Statement** whose subject digests the evidence bundle, register it to your Transparency Service, and ship the **SCITT Receipt** with it → any third party verifies authenticity, contents, issuer, and log-inclusion **offline, forever**, using open standards rather than your proprietary format. First \"**standards-native proof-carrying security finding**.\" ([SCITT draft-22](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-scitt-architecture/), [in-toto attestation](https://github.com/in-toto/attestation/blob/main/spec/v1/envelope.md), [OpenVEX](https://openssf.org/projects/openvex/))\n\n**3. A TEE-attested sovereign agent whose attestation is bound into every finding.** Run CRUCIBLE/OBSIDIAN inside **Intel TDX or AMD SEV-SNP** (plus **NVIDIA H100-CC** if the oracle model is GPU-served), release the agent's signing key only via **attestation-gated key release**, and embed the **attestation measurement hash into each checkpoint and PCF-cert**. Every finding then proves *\"produced by this exact, unmodified agent on genuine trusted hardware.\"* Note maturity honestly (TEE.Fail) and let the witnessed log backstop it. First **cryptographically-attested autonomous offensive agent**. ([NVIDIA CC](https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/confidential-computing-on-h100-gpus-for-secure-and-trustworthy-ai/), [Anthropic Confidential Inference](https://www.anthropic.com/research/confidential-inference-trusted-vms), [TDX vs SEV-SNP](https://onidel.com/blog/amd-sev-snp-vs-intel-tdx-vps))\n\n**4. Threshold-gated destructive actions in WARDEN, with the authorization itself logged.** Require **FROST (RFC 9591) m-of-n** — agent-enclave key + human **YubiKey/HSM** co-signer + policy key — for any destructive action, add a **dormant-authorization dead-man's-switch** to bound autonomy, and **write each authorization as a signed statement into the witnessed spine**. Property: *no single point (human or agent) can authorize destruction, and every authorization is tamper-evident and non-repudiable.* ([FROST RFC 9591](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9591), [NIST threshold](https://csrc.nist.gov/projects/threshold-cryptography), [dormant authorization](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.07933))\n\n**5. Proof-of-inference receipts on the oracle step (attested now, zkML for the crown jewels).** Attach a **TEE-attested inference receipt** (model + runtime + system-prompt hash) to every oracle-confirmed finding today; for the specific classifier that gates a destructive action, pilot a **DeepProve zkML proof** so the *AI judgment itself* is cryptographically verifiable without exposing weights. This closes the last \"trust me, the model said so\" gap and makes the finding's *reasoning* provable, not just its *logging*. ([DeepProve](https://lagrange.dev/blog/deepprove-1), [github.com/Lagrange-Labs/deep-prove](https://github.com/Lagrange-Labs/deep-prove), [Azure Confidential AI Inferencing](https://next.redhat.com/2025/10/23/enhancing-ai-inference-security-with-confidential-computing-a-path-to-private-data-inference-with-proprietary-llms/))\n\n**Combined, the story becomes:** *a sovereign, TEE-attested offensive agent whose every oracle-confirmed finding is a standards-native, proof-carrying attestation, appended to a witnessed, split-view-resistant, Bitcoin-anchored transparency log, where destructive actions require threshold multi-party authorization that is itself publicly auditable.* Each clause maps to a mature-or-emerging standard above — and no existing system combines all of them.\n\n---\n\n### Honest maturity caveats to design around\n- **Witness networks** are the least-mature link (Rekor v2 shipped without witnessing) → you may need to bootstrap your own witness quorum with customers.\n- **TEE attestation is physically defeatable** (TEE.Fail, CCS 2025) → never let it stand alone; the witnessed log must be the ultimate arbiter.\n- **zkML for large LLMs is still minutes-to-hours** → use attested inference now, reserve zkML for small, high-stakes gating models.\n- **C2PA/attestation proves provenance, not truth** → the *oracle* (your veracity layer) is what makes the claim true; provenance only makes it *attributable and tamper-evident*.\n\n**Sources (primary):** [RFC 6962](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6962.html) · [Sigstore Rekor](https://github.com/sigstore/rekor) · [Rekor v2 GA](https://blog.sigstore.dev/rekor-v2-ga/) · [Trillian Witness](https://github.com/transparency-dev/armored-witness) · [Go checksum DB](https://go.dev/blog/module-mirror-launch) · [Apple iMessage
